He will be a staff for the righteous with which for them to stand and not to [[FALL]], and he will be the light of the nations and the hope of those whose hearts are troubled. All who dwell on the earth will [[FALL]] down and worship him, and they will praise and bless and celebrate with song the [[Lord of Spirits]].
- [[1 Enoch]], chapter 48, verses 4 through 5

The modern world doesn't acknowledge but is nevertheless haunted by spirits, [[Angels]], [[Demons]], and [[Saints]]. In our time, many yearn to break free of the prison of a flat, secular materialism, to see and to know reality as it truly is. What is this spiritual reality like? How do we engage with it well? How do we permeate everyday life with spiritual presence? [[Orthodox]] [[Christian]] [[Priests]], [[Father Andrew Stephen Damick]] and [[Father Stephen De Young]], host this live call-in show, focused on enchantment in creation, the union of the seen and unseen as made by [[God]] and experienced by mankind throughout history. Welcome to the [[Lord of Spirits]].

title: "The Mountain of [[God]] and the Boat of Theseus"

And it was it was simply it was a matter of stress. So I don't even remember what the word was. So uh you know, I can't even think what the word was. Um so, you know, the person said it with like say stressing the first syllable and then and then unstressing the second, whereas it should have been the other way around, right? So like for instance, the word, I don't know why this popped in my head, but dismay, right? I think they would have said dismay, you know, I I was dismayed. And um and I said, no, it's dismay. And and this other person said, that's the same. And I was like, you don't hear a difference between dismay and dismay? And they said, you just said the same word exactly the same way two times. And I and I was I was just baffled at that for whatever reason they just did not perceive that. They just did not perceive the stress in in that word. And and you know, you know, as you said, you can be taught to perceive things like that and I think it's one of the reasons why it can be difficult, especially as an adult to learn another language because you just don't you don't perceive what sounds are important and different, you know, to speakers of that language or sometimes there are our perception is submerged, right? So for instance, a classic example in English is the words keel and cool. Well, if you feel what your tongue does at the beginning of each of those words, you can tell it's in a different place, but English speakers perceive those as starting with the same sound, whereas there are other languages which those are distinctly different consonants, right? Um, so as as another example, you know, not visual, but in this case, um, aural, right?

But what this means is, what this means is that wherever [[God]]'s [[Paradise]] is, wherever the dwelling place of [[God]] is, that place is going to be under siege by the enemies of [[God]], by the spiritual enemies of [[God]]. If the [[Holy Spirit]] is dwelling within us and we're the [[Temple]] of [[God]] and we're the dwelling place of [[God]] and we become [[Paradise]] within, and and the mountain of [[God]] within, then that means there's going to be a siege. There's we're going to be under attack by [[God]]'s enemies.

Yeah. Yeah. That [[Paradise]] is within you, the the mountain of [[God]] is within you, and you're going to be under siege by the enemies of [[God]], not just because people get excited when we talk about Amalekites. Yeah, yeah. He was a pastor for decades at uh St. [[Michael]]'s Church in uh Louisville, Kentucky. Um he said, "Make the place you're in into [[Paradise]]." Right? And the specific context at the time was, you know, the the parish where [[God]] has put you as a pastor, make it as paradisaical as possible, right? But that's not the only application of that, okay? So if the kingdom of [[God]] is within you, and and again, this this emphasizes this sort of as it's called this eschatological tension of both now and not yet. So, so you, if you're a, if you are a baptized [[Christian]], then you are the place of [[Paradise]] because you've put on [[Christ]], right? As many have been baptized in [[Christ]], have put on [[Christ]]. And if [[Christ]] is the [[Temple]] of [[God]], if he is [[Paradise]], then it's within you. Um, so if you've done that and you are the [[Paradise]] of [[God]], but you also are not yet because we're we're still on the journey, right? Um, it's both now and not yet. Um, become the thing that you are. That means that spiritual life is about making yourself paradisaical. This is one of the ways of talking about it. It's not the only way. And so, so how do you do that? Right? Well, what is appropriate for a [[Temple]]? What is appropriate for [[Paradise]]? What does that look like? What do you do there? You know, number one, you are there to be with [[God]]. You are there to commune with [[God]]. You are there to worship [[God]]. That's what a [[Temple]] is. That's what [[Paradise]] is. It's the place where [[Adam]] meets [[God]] and where he is one with [[God]] and he is in [[Communion]] with [[God]]. And so that means that prayer and worship are utterly core to our to who we are. It's not just sort of something that helps with spiritual life or something we, you know, I make sure I try to do that every so often. It doesn't help, it is who we are. Right? But also, you know, think about all these things we've talked about in terms of [[Sacrifice]] uh in previous episodes and about purification of the [[Temple]] of [[God]] and and all of that, right? That also applies to you, to to who you are, right? All of these things that are what the way that a [[Temple]] functions and is treated and people go there and do those things, all of that is who you are. And even like this this image of pilgrimage, right? We started with that when we started talking about [[Sacrifice]]. There is an internal pilgrimage that you make within, which we participate in by making external pilgrimage. And by that I can mean, you can mean, you know, going on a long journey, but it could also mean simply the pilgrimage you take when you get up on a Sunday morning and go to your church, even if you live right next door to it. But also is the pilgrimage that you take when you wake up and you walk and stand in front of your [[Icons]] in the morning and at night, you're making that pilgrimage to this holy place because you are [[Paradise]] and you are making yourself paradisaical by then going to, you know, another [[Paradise]] so to speak within your home, within your community, with you know, all of these things, you know, it's it's it's um it it sort of stacks, you know, there's paradises upon [[Paradise]], right? And why do we go to the [[Saints]]? Because the [[Saints]] have become [[Paradise]]. We go there because we meet with [[God]] there and we connect with [[God]] there and commune with [[God]] there. You know, so it all of this reverberates just beautifully, beautifully, right? And if we if we, even with these sort of difficult metaphysical concepts that we began with about, you know, being a human doing, uh you know, that being is a verb, right? That figures into this very, very deeply. And if you if you understand this the right way and you're oriented the right way, then you'll then you'll know that it's not a matter of like, am I good enough? Have I done enough? Did I go to church enough? Did I love my neighbor enough or whatever? Because again, that's a very sort of quantitative approach to these things. The question is, what am how am I being? How am I being? You know, am I fundamentally oriented towards [[God]] and going to that mountain of assembly to meet with him? Right? That's, I I sometimes tell people it's not a matter of of progress or distance or arrival, it's of sort of vector, so to speak, to use a mathematical term. You know, what direction are you moving in? And that's spiritual life from this particular angle. So I I I love this conversation on sacred geography and, you know, like the first place I go is this this question that Laura talked about. You know, when I learned about the idea of thin places where I was like, whoa, there's places you can go where you can meet [[God]]. And the answer, of course, is yes. So, um my my prayer is that um that all of this will help to contribute to your to your [[Paradise]], which is not a separate one from the [[Paradise]]. **[[Fr. Stephen]]:** [Listen from 1:24:38](https://media.ancientfaith.com/audio/podcasts/lordofspirits/the_mountain_of_god_2021-03-25.mp3#t=1:24:38)
Um, you know, I I know we have a fair number of listeners who aren't uh, necessarily Americans, but I think this may apply beyond America. But I could I could say for us, Mericans, um, our heroes uh, are individuals, right? Our heroes are lone wolves, you know, the tough cop who plays by his own set of rules, you know. Um, the the, you know, you you don't you don't see a lot of TV shows and movies about people who do things by the book, you know, and sort of do a solid [[Job]], you know. It's it's, um, we all want to be individuals and achieve like individual excellence. That's what we're oriented toward. Um, but one of the consequences of everything we've been talking about tonight, but especially in terms of identity and the time we spent talking about identity and what makes something what it is, including what makes you and I who you and I are, is that it's not just about us being put in order. It's not just about us, right? It's not just an internal thing of being justified and set in order and properly arranged. Though this tends to be the focus of a lot of our [[Christian]] life and thought, is I need to get my stuff together, right? I need to get myself straightened out. I need to repent of my sins and get myself okay so that I go to heaven uh when I die and I need to find [[Salvation]] for myself. Um and that could even just be, I need to become a [[Saint]]. I need to achieve some kind of spiritual excellence, some kind of spiritual level. But just being set in order, just being a certain thing isn't your identity. Your identity includes and is dependent on that web of relationships we were talking about. The way we fit into the rest of creation, including our fellow humans, but going beyond our fellow humans to to all of the rest of creation, having that set of relationships, those connections being right, being set right, being put in order and in their proper order. Without that, we can't be internally set right. Uh this is why all through the scriptures, famously in first [[John]], but all through the scriptures, right? You can't love [[God]] and hate your neighbor. You can't have things right with [[God]] and have things be out of whack at the same time. Um, I don't want to pick on him too much, but I'm sorry, [[Martin Luther]]. You can't be just and a sinner at the same time. You just can't. So we have to always remember and especially now during [[Great Lent]], when we're we're really focused on this, we focus on [[Repentance]], that this is not just a move inward. Everything that we said about [[Paradise]] being within you and the kingdom being within you is absolutely true. But that doesn't mean that you fold in on yourself and become a solipsist and become entirely self-focused. Because finding yourself and finding what is within you is only going to happen through correcting that web of relationships. You're going to find yourself in other people. You're going to find [[God]] in other people. And when we're we're loving our neighbor and we're setting things right and we're making amends as part of our [[Repentance]] and we're putting everything around us back into order, that's what's going to cause all those things within us that are out of joint and out of place to slide back into place. That's where things are going to come together and we're going to find out who we really are and what it really means to be a human and thereby by discovering what it means to really be human, to discover what it is to be like [[Christ]] and to be like [[God]], which is what we call [[Theosis]] and what [[Salvation]] is really all about. It's about becoming a [[Son of God]] as we've said before, becoming like him, becoming like [[Christ]]. That doesn't happen for us all alone on our own. That happens for with for us as part of the church, as part of a community of the faithful, both those who are alive with us in this world now and those who have gone before. We all find [[Salvation]] and restoration and [[Paradise]] together.
